INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (70,1,'2007-05-08 20:28:34','2007-05-09 01:28:34','Photo Paper','Photo Paper',0,'','inherit','open','open','','photo-paper-2','','','2007-05-08 20:28:34','2007-05-09 01:28:34','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step05.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(71,1,'2007-05-08 20:29:50','2007-05-09 01:29:50','Printed Flames','Printed Flames',0,'','inherit','open','open','','printed-flames','','','2007-05-08 20:29:50','2007-05-09 01:29:50','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step06.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(72,1,'2007-05-08 20:30:44','2007-05-09 01:30:44','Masked','Masked',0,'','inherit','open','open','','masked','','','2007-05-08 20:30:44','2007-05-09 01:30:44','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step07.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(73,1,'2007-05-08 20:32:01','2007-05-09 01:32:01','First Coat','First Coat',0,'','inherit','open','open','','first-coat','','','2007-05-08 20:32:01','2007-05-09 01:32:01','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step08.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (74,1,'2007-05-08 20:33:07','2007-05-09 01:33:07','Clear Coat','Clear Coat',0,'','inherit','open','open','','clear-coat','','','2007-05-08 20:33:07','2007-05-09 01:33:07','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step09.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(75,1,'2007-05-08 20:33:43','2007-05-09 01:33:43','Complete','Complete',0,'','inherit','open','open','','complete-2','','','2007-05-08 20:33:43','2007-05-09 01:33:43','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step10.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(76,1,'2007-05-08 20:34:04','2007-05-09 01:34:04','Complete','Complete',0,'','inherit','open','open','','complete-3','','','2007-05-08 20:34:04','2007-05-09 01:34:04','',-1178673428,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/flames_step11.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(77,5,'2003-06-23 21:00:06','2003-06-24 02:00:06','I\'ve been doing this method on a few cars now and settled on this being my standard for all my cars when increasing the range. With the method outlined below I can achieve 10 meters (approx 30 feet) indoors.\r\n\r\nFirst of all, here\'s my stock MS 49Mhz with a Skyline shell and crapola antenna, it will be the guinea pig for today\'s little exercise...\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step01.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step01.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow let\'s remove the shell and carefully prize the plastic pcb cover off. For all you Aussie Compact Char-G owners the pcb cover is actually glued so be extra careful. The M?Sizers don\'t suffer from this, dunno about Bit Char-G.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step02.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step02.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNext, let\'s locate the nasty \'lil bugger, you will see it outlined in red below. NOTE: This is in every car I have seen to date. Unlike the crip cap in the TX which varies at the best of times. Upon closer inspection you will notice that the solder work done on this surface mount capacitor is messy and not as clean a job as the rest of the circuit board. There are theories going around that TOMY added this cap after production due to the range being too good... go figure...\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step03.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step03.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nPrize open the pcb from the chassis CAREFULLY, you don\'t want to break anything, especially little steering wires... This is done from the side OPPOSITE to all the solder pads that say L R B F V+.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step04.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step04.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOnce you have opened the chassis up sufficiently, flip it over and secure it to your bench top using some plastic putty stuff, I\'m using Bluetac in this example. You will need the pcb to be secure to give you the leverage you will need to continue...\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step05.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step05.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow, here\'s the trickiest bit of the whole procedure. Make sure you have the capacitor in the grip of some needle-nose pliers, using a soldering iron heat of the solder pads on each side of the capacitor and slowly work it loose. Be careful to not damage other components close by.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step06.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step06.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAhhhh, now isn\'t that better? See below. No more @#$%^ cap!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step07.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step07.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nLook how bloody small the thing is, shown here next to a steering knuckle.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step08.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step08.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step08.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow that the hard parts done, let\'s add a new antenna. For those people paying attention I had removed it earlier. Shown below is some enameled wire (0.25mm dia) I\'m using for an antenna, it works well for its purpose...\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step09.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step09.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step09.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step09.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nRemember to always TIN your wire first to allow for an easier solder job when connecting it back onto the pcb. I cannot stress this enough.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step10.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step10.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step10.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step10.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAnd you\'re done...\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step11.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step11.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step11.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step11.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nWith the cripple capacitor removed and a new antenna added, we\'re ready to hit the town.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step12.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step12.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step12.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step12.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNot without our crew though!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step13.jpg\' title=\'crip_cap_step13.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/crip_cap_step13.jpg\' alt=\'crip_cap_step13.jpg\' /></a>','The \'Crip Cap\' (In The Car) Mod',0,'','publish','open','open','','the-crip-cap-in-the-car-mod','','','2008-01-10 18:43:17','2008-01-11 00:43:17','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/06/23/the-crip-cap-in-the-car-mod/',0,'post','',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (93,5,'2003-06-27 23:28:22','2003-06-28 04:28:22','This is a mod I have done to be able to double the charge time on your controller. It is good for those times when you\'re out in the trenches and you need an extra charge. This was done on the Aussie release of Bits known as the Compact Char-G.\r\n\r\nHere\'s how it is done:\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nFirst, open up the case of the controller. The screws are located in the locations marked by the red boxes.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step01.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step01.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nBe careful how you open it, there are very small and fine wires inside that connect the battery/charger and antenna to the main board. The side with the antenna is the side that acts like a hinge when you open the controller. It won\'t open the other way, the wires won\'t allow it.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step02.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step02.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nRemove the two screws marked in the red boxes to be able to remove the PCB from the casing.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step03.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step03.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow lets find the timing cap, this is the part we want to replace with another cap of greater value. You can see below the picture showing two black canisters. The one on the left (marked as C12) is the one we want to remove. These parts are known as electrolytic capacitors.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step04.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step04.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow the original cap is marked as being a 47uF, that\'s microfarads people. For this exercise I\'ve decided to change the 47uF to a 100uF cap.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step05.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step05.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTo remove the old cap locate the solder pads on the underside of the PCB and melt them with a soldering iron. Pull out the opposite leg of the cap each at a time and you will have a set of solder pads that look like this, nice and clean and ready for new solder......\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step06.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step06.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou can see from the pic below that the -ve terminal is \"meshed\" so one can differentiate between -ve and +ve terminals, this is needed because the capacitors we are using only work in the one voltage/current direction.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step07.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step07.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTaking note of the polarity of the capacitor make sure you put it in the right way, making sure that +ve and GND are all ok.\r\n\r\nHere\'s the finished product.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step08.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step08.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step08.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAnd no one will know the difference, below you can see the cap through the semi-clear plastic covering of the controller.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step09.jpg\' title=\'controller_time_step09.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step09.jpg\' alt=\'controller_time_step09.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe capacitor I chose was based on a very quick calculation. The original cap was 47uF, this factored to a 53 sec charge time. I replaced with a 100uF cap and got a 2 minute charge.\r\n\r\nI\'m yet to test how this affects available runtime, will post info soon.','Extend Your Controller\'s Charging Time X 2',0,'','publish','open','open','','extend-your-controllers-charging-time-x-2','','','2008-01-10 18:43:00','2008-01-11 00:43:00','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/06/27/extend-your-controllers-charging-time-x-2/',0,'post','',0),(94,1,'2007-05-08 23:30:30','2007-05-09 04:30:30','','controller_time_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','controller_time_step03jpg','','','2007-05-08 23:30:30','2007-05-09 04:30:30','',-1178684902,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/controller_time_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (101,1,'2003-07-06 12:40:17','2003-07-06 17:40:17','Along the way in this tutorial, we\'ll not only discuss how to install the alloy rims, but we\'ll note a few of the differences between the rims themselves - as well as the various suggested installation methods. Now let\'s begin.\r\n\r\nFirst, let\'s take a look at the parts we\'ll be needing to get the job done*:\r\n(the asterisk denotes a difference, we\'ll discuss this just below)\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step01.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step01.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<ul>\r\n	<li>4 rim rings</li>\r\n	<li>4 rim backings</li>\r\n	<li>4 hubs</li>\r\n	<li>4 rotors and calipers</li>\r\n	<li>4 tires</li>\r\n	<li>set of knuckles</li>\r\n	<li>rear axle and gear</li>\r\n</ul>\r\n\r\nThis is where we might run in to our first differences. As noted above, we have 4 of each component that makes up our set of alloy rims. The list and image above are of 10 mm rims, as such, other styles and sizes may come with more or less components. For example, one may get a set of rims where the rings and backings are all in one piece. These differences are minor and insignificant to the basic installation methods for the most part.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step02.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step02.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step02.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>Next, let\'s take a look at the tools we\'re going to need to get the job done:\r\n(the asterisk denotes a difference, we\'ll discuss this just below)\r\n\r\n<ul>\r\n	<li>epoxy resin, putty, etc. or paper *</li>\r\n	<li>super glue or an equivalent</li>\r\n</ul>\r\n\r\nHere\'s another spot where we might run in to some differences in installation methods, as we\'ll find out more later in Part 2 (preparing the parts for mounting) of this article. We can choose to use epoxy resin, putty, etc. or paper. I recommend epoxy, that is what I used and have had successful results - therefore we\'ll be showing and explaining the \"fill\" (epoxy resin, putty, etc.) method rather than the \"wrap\" (paper, tape, etc.) method. If you would like to use the wrap method, you can read about it being used and how to do it in our <a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/02/26/rollin-on-dubs-thanks-to-pullbacks/\">Rollin\' On Dubs Thanks To Pullbacks</a> article.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-2-preparing-parts-mounting-front-rims/\">Next - Part 2: Preparing Parts & Mounting Front Rims</a>','Alloy Rim Installation - Part 1: Parts & Tools To Get The Job Done',0,'','publish','open','open','','alloy-rim-installation-part-1-parts-tools-to-get-the-job-done','','','2007-08-19 20:41:28','2007-08-20 01:41:28','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-1-parts-tools-to-get-the-job-done/',0,'post','',0),(102,1,'2007-05-09 12:44:14','2007-05-09 17:44:14','','alloy_rims_step01.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','alloy_rims_step01jpg','','','2007-05-09 12:44:14','2007-05-09 17:44:14','',-1178732417,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (103,1,'2007-05-09 12:46:49','2007-05-09 17:46:49','','alloy_rims_step02.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','alloy_rims_step02jpg','','','2007-05-09 12:46:49','2007-05-09 17:46:49','',-1178732417,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step02.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(104,1,'2007-05-09 12:58:12','2007-05-09 17:58:12','','alloy_rims_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','alloy_rims_step03jpg','','','2007-05-09 12:58:12','2007-05-09 17:58:12','',105,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(105,1,'2003-07-06 12:56:13','2003-07-06 17:56:13','Looking at the holes in the backing of the rims, we see the holes are much too big for the rear axle or the front pins. Because we want different functions for the front and rear set of rims, we\'ll start off with the rear set and make our way up to the front set as we go along.\r\n\r\nFirst things first, we\'re going to need to fill these holes on all 4 rim backings with epoxy, like show in the image. Make sure it\'s filled completely with no air pockets in it, trim any excess with the hobby knife and leave over night to dry completely.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step03.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step03.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step03.jpg\' /></a><a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step04.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step04.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOnce dried, take the small drill bit and drill the new holes for the pins and axle to fit in to. It is very important that we drill these holes straight, or straight as we can - if we don\'t, we\'ll create a \"wabble\". A drill press creates the optimal results, but you can probably improvise and use a vise or something similar.\r\n\r\nNow that we have the holes drilled, we\'re going to focus on the rear axle and the rims to go on them. First, let\'s note that one of our rear rims needs a nub on the outside of the rim backing to allow the rim to set away from the chassis just a tad, just like on our normal rims. Here\'s another fork in the road for installation, we have two options here. We can choose to cut off the nub on the rim we previously took off the axle for this mod, or we can choose to flip the rim backing and try to use that as our nub. I recommend the first method, creating a nub, rather than the \"flip\" method and using the opposite side of the rim backing. I have my arguments but we won?t go in to those, the \"flip\" method is self explanatory so we\'ll focus on creating our own nub.\r\n\r\nCut the nub off the rim we previously took off the axle for this mod, making sure to cut straight and clean, we\'re going to be mounting this to the backside of the alloy rim directly so we want a nice smooth, even surface. Put the nub on the axle temporarily and now slide the rim backing on to the axle as well, again, making sure the rim is on straight and evenly. Take a toothpick and dab it in super glue, touch it to the under part of the nub that will contact the rim backing. Slide the nub down on to the rim backing, spin and move the axle back and forth while the super glue dries so we don?t accidentally glue the nub and rim to the axle.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step05.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step05.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step05.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>Now let\'s focus on the front set of rim backings. Mount the rim to the pin, we want the rim to spin freely, smoothly, and evenly on the pin. If we notice that any of these are not true, then we\'ve got just a little fine tuning to do. Just as we straightened out the holes for the rear set by filing with our drill bit, we can do the same for the front set. Again, our focus here is to get the rim to spin freely, smoothly, and evenly - no wabbles. Once we\'re satisfied, we can just finish mounting the front rims by pushing the pins with the rim backings in place, back on to the knuckles. Make sure not to leave too much pin sticking out the rim backing or we won\'t be able to mount our hub correctly. Make sure everything spins freely, smoothly, and evenly again.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-3-mounting-rear-rims/\">Next - Part 3: Mounting Rear Rims</a>','Alloy Rim Installation - Part 2: Preparing Parts & Mounting Front Rims',0,'','publish','open','open','','alloy-rim-installation-part-2-preparing-parts-mounting-front-rims','','','2007-08-19 20:41:12','2007-08-20 01:41:12','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-2-preparing-parts-mounting-front-rims/',0,'post','',0),(106,1,'2007-05-09 12:58:38','2007-05-09 17:58:38','','alloy_rims_step04.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','alloy_rims_step04jpg','','','2007-05-09 12:58:38','2007-05-09 17:58:38','',-1178733373,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step04.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

First, let\'s slide our gear back on to the axle, leave it at center - it\'s okay if it floats around for now. Grab a rim backing with the pre-drilled hole and fit it on to the axle just up to where the tip of the axle is flush with the front of the rim backing. We may need to take our drill bit and file the holes bigger or more straight. If our holes get to big and allows the rim to slip on and off the axle too easily, just use a little piece of paper or tape like in the \"wrap\" method to shim it up and tighten the fit. Our goal is to get the rim backing as straight as possible when mounting, and make sure it\'s tight fitting. When we are satisfied, take the rim back off, dip the tip of the axle in super glue and mount the rim and let it dry. Now that the rim has dried on to the axle, let\'s go ahead and glue the gear on to the rim backing. As always, make sure it is even.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nNow we\'re down to our last rim to mount, the \"nubbed\" rim. Just as before, we need to make sure when we mount the rim to the axle, we have a tight and even fit, we can use our drill bit a file it just like before if we have to. Also, if our hole becomes too big, we can use a bit of the \"wrap\" method just as before to help us obtain a tighter, more even fit. Once satisfied, we need to do only one thing before we glue the rim on to the axle - we need to get our spacing between both rim backings correct or we\'ll be left with a useless axle with cool rims! To get the spacing correct, put the nubbed rim on temporarily and snap the axle in to place in the chassis. Move the nubbed rim as needed, we want just a few millimeters of play in between the rims and chassis on both sides. Once satisfied with our placement, we can pop the axle back out of the chassis of the car and take a piece of tape and wrap it around the axle, butting it right up to the nub on the rim. We\'re marking our placement here, now pull the rim off and dip the end of the axle in some super glue - mount the rim with nub butting right up next to the tape on the axle. Let it dry and remove the tape. We can do a test roll by just pushing the axle with our finger, it should roll evenly and with no wabbles. If we\'ve used a stock axle, we shouldn\'t have any overhang. If we do have overhang, clip it off right at the front edge of the rim, don\'t leave any hanging out - it looks ugly and could give us problems when mounting the hubs.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-4-finishing-touches/\">Next - Part 4: Finishing Touches</a>','Alloy Rim Installation - Part 3: Mounting Rear Rims',0,'','publish','open','open','','alloy-rim-installation-part-3-mounting-rear-rims','','','2007-08-19 20:40:44','2007-08-20 01:40:44','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-3-mounting-rear-rims/',0,'post','',0),(110,1,'2007-05-09 13:49:41','2007-05-09 18:49:41','','alloy_rims_step07.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','alloy_rims_step07jpg','','','2007-05-09 13:49:41','2007-05-09 18:49:41','',111,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step07.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (111,1,'2003-07-06 13:08:09','2003-07-06 18:08:09','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step07.jpg\' title=\'alloy_rims_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/alloy_rims_step07.jpg\' alt=\'alloy_rims_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nNow that we have all our rim backings mounted and in place, the rest is a cake walk. First, if we\'re going to paint our hubs, keep them intact and in place on their holders - they\'re easier to paint this way. Once we\'re ready to mount, clip the hubs and rotors out of their holders. Bend the brake caliper over so we have just a round rotor with a caliper over it, put a few dabs on the inside edge on the back of the rotor and mount it to the rim backing, do this on all four rims. Grab our rim rings and do the same, put a few dabs of glue on them back side of the ring and mount them to all four rim backings. Now grab the hubs, and again do the same thing, put a few dabs of glue on the outer edge of the hub and push them in to place inside the rim - use the end of a pencil with an un-used eraser end to push and hold them in place while they dry.\r\n\r\nWe\'re done, all we need to do now is put the tires on rims and give it a test. We may notice some of our tires are much too big, we have two more options at this point. We can choose to try and shave down the tires that came with the alloy rims, or we can choose to try and use a tire from a set of Pullbacks (as shown here). I choose to do a combination of both. I shaved down my tires for the front set, and used rear pullback tires for my rear alloy rims - seems to be a perfect combination for how I like to drive. I should also mention that my rims are 10.0 mm rims, so the rear pullback tires fit perfectly, smaller alloy rims may have some ill fitting issues - I\'m not certain.\r\n\r\nNow lets go bling our sled and floss it\'s new shoes homie!\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpGallery/albums/userpics/10008/gtrr34_silver_fade_05.jpg\"><img src=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpGallery/albums/userpics/10008/gtrr34_silver_fade_05.jpg\" alt=\"\" /></a>','Alloy Rim Installation - Part 4: Finishing Touches',0,'','publish','open','open','','alloy-rim-installation-part-4-finishing-touches','','','2007-08-19 20:40:21','2007-08-20 01:40:21','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/07/06/alloy-rim-installation-part-4-finishing-touches/',0,'post','',0),(112,6,'2003-07-28 18:38:16','2003-07-28 23:38:16','<strong>Materials/Tools needed:</strong>\r\n<ul>\r\n	<li>3 of the some type of Bit/Zip/MS batterys</li>\r\n	<li>Wire</li>\r\n	<li>Electrical tape</li>\r\n	<li>Soldering iron/pen/gun</li>\r\n	<li>Solder</li>\r\n	<li>Something to remove a battery (EG:Smal screwdriver,guitar pick, and so on)</li>\r\n</ul>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/triplecell_diagram.jpg\' title=\'Triple Cell Diagram\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/triplecell_diagram.jpg\' alt=\'Triple Cell Diagram\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<strong>Step 1:</strong> Solder a wire between the - end of cell 2 to the + end of cell 3.\r\n<strong>Step 2:</strong> Solder a wire to the + end of cell 2.\r\n<strong>Step 3:</strong> Solder a wire to the - end of cell 3.\r\n<strong>Step 4:</strong> Tape (or solder then cover with tape for extra hold) the wire from the + end of cell 2 to the - end of cell 1. INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (113,1,'2007-05-09 18:45:16','2007-05-09 23:45:16','Triple Cell Diagram','Triple Cell Diagram',0,'','inherit','open','open','','triple-cell-diagram','','','2007-05-09 18:45:16','2007-05-09 23:45:16','',-1178753896,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/triplecell_diagram.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(114,7,'2003-09-09 19:46:58','2003-09-10 00:46:58','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step01.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step01.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step01.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>This body mod will turn 1 1/2 Nissan Gloria bodies into a Elcamino style truck to fit a ZipZap Chassis. This project can take a week to do and not recomended to do on a weekend for best quality.\r\n\r\nWith that said let\'s get all the supplies needed for this project.\r\nYou will need CA Glue (Super Glue), Two Gloria Bodies (Or any two like car bodies), #11 X-acto Blade, a set of needle files, various sanding films (Sandpaper on plastic film for wet sanding), some type of putty, sheets of plastic, and compatiable paints of your choice (1 as a primer).\r\n<strong>You can also use a micro saw inplace of the X-acto.</strong>\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step02.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step02.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nTime to start cutting SLOWLY. We will be cutting the back half of the body off.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step03.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step03.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step03.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>To cut using an Xacto blade turn the blade over and use the back side of the tip of the blade. Scrapping slowly in a pulling motion with the sharp edge of the blade facing out. Slowly scratch the body along the door seam, the body line, and a straight line from the body line to the bottom of the body. As marked the the picture.\r\nNote: Take your time and go slow with this part.\r\n\r\nNext score between the bottom of the back side window toward the back of the body till you get to the trunk. Repeat this process on both sides. Now remove the back window using the same scoring method starting at the top of the window working our way down each side. As you get close to cutting through the sides go real slow as at this point the back window should now be to only thing holding the body together. With the back half off use the trunk seam and the two holes from the removed spoiler as you guide and remove the rear deck. Your parts should look like this:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step04.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step04.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step05.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step05.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step05.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>Now we are going to shorten the roof. A Traditional Elcamino is only a two door and we have a four door roof line. Cut the roof in half along the window/door seam and accross. Make sure as you are cutting the body top from side to side you go as straight as possable. Shorten the section we just removed by about a 1/4 of an inch keeping the angle the same as to was attached.\r\n\r\nWith all cutting on this body done we can start glueing. Reattach the roof that we sectioned off. Test fit it first before attaching with the CA. The two parts may need minor sanding or fileing to each other untill you get a perfect match. While that part is hardening attach the rear quarter panels shifting them about 1/32 of an inch forward.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step06.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step06.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<strong>Note: to make sure of the placement set the rear section on a ZipZap chassis and see where the panel needs to to attached.</strong>\r\n\r\nWhen all glue has hardened place the two halves on a ZZ Chassis.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step07.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step07.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<strong>Note: You may need to remove the clear cover from the pc board on the chassis to make the body fit.</strong>\r\n\r\nNow for the 1/2 Gloria body part. From the donor body we will need the whole side panel with the door seams. Measure the gap between the front half and back half. Remove the amount we need from another gloria body the same way we dismantaled the frist body. Attach the side panels the same way we did the roof sanding each edge to get as perfect a fit as possiable. Rounding the outside edge at the door seam just before you attach to create the door seam. You may have to gently widen the rear roof section as you are glueing it up to make sure it attaches properly.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step08.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step08.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step08.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step09.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step09.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step09.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step09.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>We need to build up the back end so the body will sit properly on a ZZ chassis and clear the PC board. We will add a 1/16\" strip of plastic aroud the side and back top edge. It was .040\" thick Styrene found at a hobby shop. Also cut a rectangle (back window) wider than the old back window about 3/8\" tall. Glue that rectangle under the body straight up just at the point of the body before it bends. Test fit again to make sure the new rear window does not touch the chassis. If it does gently file or sand the window till it sits properly.\r\n\r\n<strong>Gray mark inside body</strong>\r\nMake two triangle shaped peices to fill the gap inside the old rear window frame. Test fit again and make sure no plastic is touching a tire or the chassis and sand or file till all parts are clear of the chasses. Take the thinest plastic you have and cut a rectangle larger than the rear window to cover the old plate and half of each tail light to make the tailgate. Glue it on at the very top edge of the bumper up. Don\'t worry if it is taller than the bed.\r\n<strong>Note: You may need to file or sand the rear end to properly seat the tailgate.</strong>\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step10.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step10.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step10.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step10.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>Take a thin peice of plastic and cut it larger than the bed but small enough to fit inside the body. Hold it inside the body and using a fine tiped marker mark the plastic where the window and the sides are from the out side. Cut on the outside of those marks and test fit. Gently remove plastic untill you get as close to perfect fit as you like it and glue in place. With one of those scraps glue a peice of plastic inside the body spaning the gap of the rear quarter panel. Smooth out all your rough edges with your needle files and your course sanding film. Use a slightly damp cloth to remove all excess dust. Fill in all gaps with putty of your choice. Leave the body sit for at least 1 whole day. Sand all putty points till roughly smooth. Sand with a progressively finer grain under water to get an even smoother finish to the body. Examine the body again after sanding and add putty to any low spots or gaps that you find. Wait again another day to sand with and even finer grain of sanding film. Take a damp cloth again and clean the body off and let it dry. Spray on a VERRY THIN layer of your primer on the body and let dry. After drying examine the body again for any inperfections and either sand them down or add putty and wait for it to dry. Once the body is at the level of smoothness you like spray it again with a thin even coat to cover all parts of the body. Paint with your choice of color paint in super light thin coats.\r\n<strong>NOTE: When masking the windows mask both the inside and outside before painting. Keep the donor body parts never know what you can make out of it.</strong>\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step11.jpg\' title=\'elcamino_step11.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step11.jpg\' alt=\'elcamino_step11.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nI painted mine white and blue and used the white as my primer. There are atleast 8+ super thin coats of white on this body and only 3 of the blue.','CJ\'s ElCamino Gloria',0,'','publish','open','open','','cjs-elcamino-gloria','','','2007-08-19 20:39:31','2007-08-20 01:39:31','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/09/cjs-elcamino-gloria/',0,'post','',0),(115,1,'2007-05-09 19:53:26','2007-05-10 00:53:26','','elcamino_step01.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','elcamino_step01jpg','','','2007-05-09 19:53:26','2007-05-10 00:53:26','',-1178758018,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (125,1,'2007-05-09 20:14:47','2007-05-10 01:14:47','','elcamino_step10.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','elcamino_step10jpg','','','2007-05-09 20:14:47','2007-05-10 01:14:47','',114,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step10.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(126,1,'2007-05-09 20:15:30','2007-05-10 01:15:30','','elcamino_step11.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','elcamino_step11jpg','','','2007-05-09 20:15:30','2007-05-10 01:15:30','',114,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/elcamino_step11.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(127,3,'2003-09-18 21:39:38','2003-09-19 02:39:38','This mod is da bomb. Thanks for PsuedoRooter for turning me on to it, and thanks to Clyston at BitMod.tk for posting this: <a href=\"http://www.universalhost.de/html_bit/antiboost.htm\" target=\"_blank\">http://www.universalhost.de/html_bit/antiboost.htm</a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<strong>What it does:</strong>\r\nIt essentailly reverses the function of the boost button, so the boost is on continously, and you press the button to slow down. The only disadvantage I can see, is that the controller is constantly putting out a signal, so you\'ll have to make sure you shut it off when your done, so you don\'t kill your batteries.\r\n\r\n<strong>What you need:</strong>\r\nA resistor, and of coarse a soldering iron, solder, and basic soldering skills.\r\n\r\n<strong>What you do:</strong>\r\nFirst you take the controller apart. Remove the PCB from the front. Your now going to be looking at the front of the board. Cut the copper strip on the left of the boost button, the - one. Now attach a small wire from the contact on the left, to where the batteries + is going. You then just attach a resistor from the right contact on the button, to the contact where the battery\'s - goes. Clystron used a 10k, but I noticed in the bottom picture(from the link above) that someone had used a 1k resistor. Since that\'s one that I had around I used that. It worked fine, so I think you just need to put a resistor there of some sort, but I would stick to the 1k-10k range to be safe. Now you just need to put your controller back together, and that\'s the hardest part.\r\n\r\n<strong>How does it work?</strong>\r\nAbsolutely fabulous. Now the controller works the way my brain does, and it\'s so much easier to use. I also noticed if it\'s gettig a running start with the boost, the un-boosted mode doesn\'t seem so slow and choppy.\r\n\r\nThis mod get my highest reccomendation, whatever that may be.\r\n\r\nSorry about the fuzzy pic. I was too lazy to take my controller apart again, to take a better one.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/antiboost_step01.jpg\' title=\'Anti-Boost\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/antiboost_step01.jpg\' alt=\'Anti-Boost\' /></a>','The Anti-Boost Mod',0,'','publish','open','open','','the-anti-boost-mod','','','2008-01-10 18:42:25','2008-01-11 00:42:25','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/18/the-anti-boost-mod/',0,'post','',0),(128,1,'2007-05-09 21:42:35','2007-05-10 02:42:35','Anti-Boost','Anti-Boost',0,'','inherit','open','open','','anti-boost','','','2007-05-09 21:42:35','2007-05-10 02:42:35','',-1178764778,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/antiboost_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (129,1,'2003-09-19 23:00:35','2003-09-20 04:00:35','Let\'s start off by answering a few of the more common questions.\r\n\r\n<strong>What type of printer should I use?</strong>\r\nIdeally, you would want to use a laser printer - they print cleaner, easier, and more precise than inkjet or bubble jet printers. Laser printers print in better detail and don\'t leave \"dots\" of ink like inkjet or bubble jet printers do. However, color laser printers can be quite expensive and you may not have access to one, so you\'re left with the inkjet or bubble jet. Using inkjet or bubblejet will only add an extra step or two to the process.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<strong>Do they sell specific decal paper for these printers?</strong>\r\nYes. You can get clear or white sheets for either laser or inkjet and bubble jet printers.\r\n\r\n<strong>Most printers don\'t print with white ink, what should I do?</strong>\r\nYou can either use white decal paper or use clear decal paper and put your decal on a white background.\r\n\r\n<strong>Do I need to add a protective coating after I print my decals?</strong>\r\nLaser printed decals do not need a protective coating, though one could never hurt. Inkjet or bubble jet printed decals do need a protective coating, otherwise you run the risk of having the decal\'s ink \"bleed\" and run. They sell protective coating specifically for decals, though you could use the same clear coat finish you would use when painting your car body, it works just as well.\r\n\r\n<strong>So what steps do I follow? How should I make my decals?</strong>\r\nFirst, you\'ll need a good graphic. Find or make your graphic, vector based graphics are much more crisp than rastor based graphics. You\'ll want to size your graphic to the size you\'ll need. Do a couple of \"test prints\" on regular paper so you can see if you need to make any adjustments.\r\n\r\nOnce you have your graphic ready and have made your test prints and are happy, just put your decal paper in your printer and print away.\r\n\r\nOnce the sheet is done printing, let the sheet sit out for awhile before you touch it, or you could smear the ink. Once completely dry, if using inkjet or bubble jet printed decals, you should add a clear coat of some sort to help protect the graphic and make it easier to apply. You can use thin coats of clear coat paint just as you would with your cars. They also seel decal clear coat specific for decals. On inkjet or bubble jet water slide decals, it is a must that you coat the \"water slide\" decals or you will get bleeding.\r\n\r\nOnce the clear coat is completely dried, you\'re ready to cut out your decals and start applying them.\r\n\r\n<strong>Are there any tips for applying \"water slide\" decals?</strong>\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=6311\" target=\"_blank\">You bet, click here to learn how.</a>','Making Your Own Decals',0,'','publish','open','open','','making-your-own-decals','','','2007-08-19 20:38:27','2007-08-20 01:38:27','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/19/making-your-own-decals/',0,'post','',0),(130,1,'2007-05-10 23:58:32','2007-05-11 04:58:32','','dip_switch_step01.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','dip_switch_step01jpg','','','2007-05-10 23:58:32','2007-05-11 04:58:32','',132,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (131,1,'2007-05-10 23:59:28','2007-05-11 04:59:28','','dip_switch_step02.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','dip_switch_step02jpg','','','2007-05-10 23:59:28','2007-05-11 04:59:28','',132,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step02.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(132,5,'2003-09-19 23:57:34','2003-09-20 04:57:34','For ages I\'ve been looking for a switch that is small enough to be able to put on my little beasts to be able to turn them ON and OFF.\r\n\r\nI know that the car doesn\'t really NEED a switch, but hey! that\'s never stopped me before. ;)\r\n\r\nBasically the goal is to be able to take the battery out of circuit and charge it without any excess current drain from the PCB/lights.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nTheoretically you should get a slightly increased runtime from the extra charge the battery would get but I believe this difference would only be small.\r\n\r\nAnother advantage is to be able to turn the car off and pick up later with no issues of battery loss.\r\n\r\nSo here it is, my DIP Switch mod.\r\n\r\n<strong>Find the right switch</strong>\r\nHere\'s a DIP switch, not much to look at hey.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step01.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step01.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThis is obviously too big, but you can saw off a single switch! I used a metal saw to do this since the cutting blade is finer and causes less damage to the surrounding area. If everything works you should have some pieces like this.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step02.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step02.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe flywheel helps give you an idea of the switch\'s size.\r\n\r\n<strong>Where do I mount the bloody thing?</strong>\r\nThe best place I found was underneath the engine well. This means the switch is out of sight, out of mind. I\'ve drilled two holes in the vertical part of the chassis adjacent to the engine well. This is where I\'ll feed to wires back to the battery.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step03.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step03.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nHere\'s a side view to help give you an idea of how the switch is mounted. You can see from this side profile that the clearance to the ground is juuuuuuuust fine, yeah!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step04.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step04.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe little metal ends of the switch were cut back till only 2mm long. I then soldered some enamelled wire to the ends. I use this wire \'cause it\'s flexible and small.\r\n\r\n<strong>If you want to use normal insulated wire that\'s fine but find some that is thin enough \'cause the wires could rub against the rear axle causing all sorts of problems.</strong>\r\n\r\nHere\'s two pictures showing the bugger all clearance between the wire and rear axle.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step05.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step05.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow feed the wires up and over one of the notches in the chassis as shown below. These notches are on all classes of legit bits.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step06.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step06.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<strong>Solder time!</strong>\r\nOk, first thing to do now is to remove the -VE/GND/NEGATIVE power wire from the PCB and battery terminal.\r\n\r\nNow solder one of the wires from the switch (doesn\'t matter which one) to the battery terminal and the other wire to the -VE terminal on the PCB.\r\n\r\nTo the right, you can see (barely!) where I soldered the wire from the DIP switch.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step07.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step07.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOnce that is done put the car back together and all should be cool.\r\nQ.E.D\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step08.jpg\' title=\'dip_switch_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step08.jpg\' alt=\'dip_switch_step08.jpg\' /></a>','The DIP Switch Mod',0,'','publish','open','open','','the-dip-switch-mod','','','2008-01-10 18:42:01','2008-01-11 00:42:01','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/19/the-dip-switch-mod/',0,'post','',0),(133,1,'2007-05-11 00:00:47','2007-05-11 05:00:47','','dip_switch_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','dip_switch_step03jpg','','','2007-05-11 00:00:47','2007-05-11 05:00:47','',-1178859454,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/dip_switch_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (140,1,'2007-05-11 14:49:23','2007-05-11 19:49:23','','charger_step02.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','charger_step02jpg','','','2007-05-11 14:49:23','2007-05-11 19:49:23','',142,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step02.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(141,1,'2007-05-11 14:50:05','2007-05-11 19:50:05','','charger_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','charger_step03jpg','','','2007-05-11 14:50:05','2007-05-11 19:50:05','',142,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(142,8,'2003-09-24 14:48:16','2003-09-24 19:48:16','I am finally getting around to posting this simple tutorial/how to. What we\'re going to do is make an AC charger out of commonly available parts with the least amount of hassle. My kind of mod! Since I didn\'t take pictures as I was building it, we\'re going to deconstruct it. Here\'s a picture of the final product:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step01.jpg\' title=\'charger_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step01.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nYou will need to make a trip to Walmart and Radio Shack. You will need a soldering iron, a little bit of wire, glue/epoxy, a charging pad from one of your many Bit Char-g or Microsizer controllers, a drill, and about an hour of time.\r\n\r\nAt Walmart, get a MotorWorks car, whichever you like, as long as it has the charging base. I like using the Motorworks base as a foundation mainly because it has a button to start the charge, so doing mulitple charges or longer charging time doesn\'t require you to change a capacitor or take the car on and off. Plus, it can operate on batteries if you\'re somewhere without electricity, and works for both single and dual cell cars. And it has a nifty stash box, er storage compartment on the bottom. Also available from Walmart, get this adapter:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step02.jpg\' title=\'charger_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step02.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nHue35 and I conducted a federally funded study over a 3 month period to determine the best adapter for charging your Bits. This one is the best. 1000mA is the rating you are looking for, though 800 will work (1500 would probably work too, but would take it\'s toll on your car batteries). It aslo does multiple voltages, so charging dual and triple cell cars only requires the flick of the switch. Be wary of Radio Shack adapters, they have build-in short circuit protection, which renders them useless for this purpose. Actually, one adapter we found that seemed to charge the cars better was a Sony 3 volt adapter that was rated at 700mA, but put out substantially more than that. Hue35 will have to add that detail, I forgot the meter rating. On the end of the cord you can see the N Adaptaplug you\'ll need to get at Radio Shack. It\'s kind of weird that Walmart and Radio Shack products work together.\r\n\r\nAt Radio Shack, get a power jack plug (M or N will do, mine\'s N I believe), and a corresponding Adaptaplug. The jack you need looks like this:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step03.jpg\' title=\'charger_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step03.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see, I drilled another hole, and started a second (third time\'s a charm!) The original hole was when I had this wired up with a male Adaptaplug connector that I plugged straight in to the power adaptor. the second hole that I started is where I wanted to put the jack. The reason it has to be where it is has to do with the location of the PCB and battery compartment inside the charging base, and the room you will need for the back end of the jack.\r\n\r\nBefore we wire it up and put the jack in, we remove the MotorWorks charging pad and replace it with a Bit-Char-g pad like this:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step04.jpg\' title=\'charger_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step04.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see, I shimmed the sides with some other plastic bits and doused it with model glue. The height is fairly critical, as in don\'t make it too low so your car\'s tires keep it from seating on the pad. I also reinforced mine on the back side, since this little piece of plastic is going to see lots of action. You can tell by how rounded off all the plastic is how many charges this base has given.\r\n\r\nOnce the glue has dried, solder the wires on to the PCB where the MotorWorks pad was attached originally. You have to keep the red/black wires going to the same places on the PCB, but it doesn\'t matter from the jack to the battery contacts. You can control polarity with the Adaptaplug by sticking it on the cable 180 degrees either way.\r\n\r\nHeres the inside:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step05.jpg\' title=\'charger_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step05.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nI left the wires from the battery compartment in place so I can still use this charging base with batteries. All you need to do is solder the tabs on the jack to the contacts where the battery leads go from the batteries to the PCB.\r\n\r\nI even rigged up a little jumper so I can do both single and dual cell cars with batteries.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step06.jpg\' title=\'charger_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step06.jpg\' alt=\'charger_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThat\'s it! Boom, done.\r\n\r\nI\'ve done tons of mods over the last 8 months, and this is by no means the flashiest, but is by far the most useful. Charging your car with the same batteries you use to transmit signal creates two problems: inconsistent charges and reduced transmitting power (= less range). Since I started using this, I haven\'t changed the batteries in my controller, and I use it quite a bit. Hue35 and I each have one of these, and it makes our charges so consistent that if we have equally conditioned batteries, we will run out of charge within 20-30 seconds of eachother. We usually double or triple charge with no ill effects to our car\'s batteries. we have a couple cars with 200mA batteries, and those take a quadruple charge, no problem. The beauty of it is just hitting that button when the light goes out (and trying desperately to count to 3 without forgetting).\r\n\r\nI may add more later, but that about covers it. Questions?','Build Your Own Charger & Stop Buying Batteries',0,'','publish','open','open','','build-your-own-charger-stop-buying-batteries','','','2008-01-10 18:41:44','2008-01-11 00:41:44','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/24/build-your-own-charger-stop-buying-batteries/',0,'post','',0),(143,1,'2007-05-11 14:50:39','2007-05-11 19:50:39','','charger_step04.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','charger_step04jpg','','','2007-05-11 14:50:39','2007-05-11 19:50:39','',-1178912896,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/charger_step04.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

Most of us revert to tape. I reverted to tape. Funtional, but I just thought it looked sloppy, and even more so I hate the tape gooies, and all the dirt they pick up.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nSo I set out to find a solution. I like to make stuff out of Zip Zap packaging because they use so much extra plastic, and I hate to pollute our land fills. Anyways I\'ve trying to work this out for a while, but I couldn\'t get the back tabs to work, but then tonight I had an ingenious idea, heres what you do:\r\n\r\nCut a piece of ZZ packaging or similar clear plastic. I like the ZZ packageing because it is several different thicknesses throughout. I used a piece of medium thick plastic from the dome.\r\n\r\nHold it over your chassis, trace the basic outline with a sharpie, make the 2 front tabs extra long, and make a tab in the back that fits in the middle gap in the chassis, (see pics) you want it to be snug, so it holds the back down. When you get it all cut out bend the front tabs down then forwards.\r\n\r\nYour done.\r\n\r\nIt\'s that simple, and I think it looks alot cleaner than tape, but it still not quite as clean as stock.\r\n\r\nCheck it out:\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/pcb_step02.jpg\' title=\'pcb_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/pcb_step02.jpg\' alt=\'pcb_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/pcb_step03.jpg\' title=\'pcb_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/pcb_step03.jpg\' alt=\'pcb_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNot bad huh?','Lo-Pro PCB Cover',0,'','publish','open','open','','lo-pro-pcb-cover','','','2007-08-19 20:37:13','2007-08-20 01:37:13','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/27/lo-pro-pcb-cover/',0,'post','',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (152,3,'2003-09-29 14:55:49','2003-09-29 19:55:49','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/grip_step01.jpg\' title=\'grip_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/grip_step01.jpg\' alt=\'grip_step01.jpg\' class=\"alignright\" /></a>My SE controller cramps my hand up, and I wanted a longer handle. So here\'s what I did, I picked up some of this Crayola Model Magic stuff from the grocery store. It\'s a modeling compound that dries into foam rubber.\r\n\r\nI used two of the individual packs that come inside. I smashed and formed it around the base of the controller, and then formed the handle part. After I had it the way I wanted, I took an exacto knife and trimmed the edge to clear the important stuff, also to give it a nice straight edge.\r\n\r\nI didn\'t have to glue it (yet). I squished about 3/4 of an inch around the bottom to hold it. Then I had to cut it away on the front and where the trigger is, and I think there is about 1/4 of an inch left in those areas.\r\n\r\nIt\'s plenty durable for regular use, but I\'m afraid one good smack will knock it off. We\'ll see.\r\n\r\nI colored it with a marker, and it looks good, and isn\'t coming off on my hand at all.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/grip_step02.jpg\' title=\'grip_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/grip_step02.jpg\' alt=\'grip_step02.jpg\' /></a>','Make A Foam Rubber Grip For Your SE Controller',0,'','publish','open','open','','make-a-foam-rubber-grip-for-your-se-controller','','','2007-05-11 14:58:16','2007-05-11 19:58:16','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2003/09/29/make-a-foam-rubber-grip-for-your-se-controller/',0,'post','',0),(153,1,'2007-05-11 15:00:25','2007-05-11 20:00:25','','lxx_fix_step01.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','lxx_fix_step01jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:00:25','2007-05-11 20:00:25','',156,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (154,1,'2007-05-11 15:01:20','2007-05-11 20:01:20','','lxx_fix_step02.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','lxx_fix_step02jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:01:20','2007-05-11 20:01:20','',156,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step02.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(155,1,'2007-05-11 15:01:40','2007-05-11 20:01:40','','lxx_fix_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','lxx_fix_step03jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:01:40','2007-05-11 20:01:40','',156,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(156,5,'2004-02-18 14:59:46','2004-02-18 19:59:46','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step01.jpg\' title=\'lxx_fix_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step01.jpg\' alt=\'lxx_fix_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nFellas, one day for no apparent reason my LXX perfection bit just stopped working. The main symptom was that the power LED on the controller turned off even though the controller was powered on. I also noticed that the batteries got very hot. There was a short somewhere on the controller PCB.\r\n\r\nAfter a bit of stuffing around and testing with my multimeter I found the problem. The power LED leads were shorting to ground!!! This basically meant that the batteries were shorted and no power was going to the controller circuit.\r\n\r\nHere\'s a pic showing the LXX perfection controller PCB, the power LED is circled in yellow.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step02.jpg\' title=\'lxx_fix_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step02.jpg\' alt=\'lxx_fix_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nHere\'s a close-up showing the source of the problem. Bloody crappy Chinese QA processes! arghhh!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step03.jpg\' title=\'lxx_fix_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/lxx_fix_step03.jpg\' alt=\'lxx_fix_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTo fix it I put a piece of rubber tire to insulate the LED pins from the PCB.\r\n\r\nAll done. Now she works without any problems. I hope this helps anyone else that has had this problem.','LXX Controller Flaw Fix',0,'','publish','open','open','','lxx-controller-flaw-fix','','','2008-01-10 18:41:25','2008-01-11 00:41:25','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/02/18/lxx-controller-flaw-fix/',0,'post','',0),(157,1,'2007-05-11 15:09:20','2007-05-11 20:09:20','','off_road_step01.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step01jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:09:20','2007-05-11 20:09:20','',161,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step01.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (158,1,'2007-05-11 15:09:33','2007-05-11 20:09:33','','off_road_step02.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step02jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:09:33','2007-05-11 20:09:33','',161,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step02.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(159,1,'2007-05-11 15:09:54','2007-05-11 20:09:54','','off_road_step03.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step03jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:09:54','2007-05-11 20:09:54','',161,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step03.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(160,1,'2007-05-11 15:10:23','2007-05-11 20:10:23','','off_road_step04.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step04jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:10:23','2007-05-11 20:10:23','',161,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step04.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(161,9,'2004-05-21 15:06:34','2004-05-21 20:06:34','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step01.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step01.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nFirst, strip your chassis of everything, except the steering coils and maybe the steering adjustor. It ends in tears if you don\'t! Tape the steering wires out of the way.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step02.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step02.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTurn the chassis upside down and cut down, straight across where the charging terminals were. This internal view shows where the blade should be. But don\'t cut all the way through.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step03.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step03.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see, straight through the charge holes. Don\'t cut through any further than this.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step04.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step04.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nStart your second cut from the top. You want to meet up with the end of your second cut. Don\'t disturb the holes for the transfer gear shaft. Do the same cut on both sides. (Well duh!)\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step05.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step05.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nIf you want to keep it as a single cell, then neaten up the edges of the cut on the front half of the chassis, and put aside.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step06.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step06.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nBut dual cells rock my world! So I remove the extra material to fit them in. it looks like too much, I know. But it\'s plenty rigid for driving. If you install a shock pivot rail (see rear shock), it will be stiffer again.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step07.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step07.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFile down the front of the motor housing until it\'s flat, no more.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step08.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step08.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step08.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nUnderneath the motor housing, the front side of the axle clip sticks down further than the rear. I just cut it down until they\'re both even. This increases clearance.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step09.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step09.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step09.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step09.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTidy up the 2nd cut marks on the motor housing and that\'s it. Try not to remove too much material from where the transfer gear sits to prevent any play. Although I\'ve removed more than this before without a problem.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step10.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step10.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step10.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step10.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nDo your best to round off all the new edges, especially the new edge on the front half of the chassis.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/22/off-road-bits-part-2-hinge-plate/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 2: Hinge Plate</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 1: Chassis Chopping',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-1-chassis-chopping','','','2008-01-10 18:41:03','2008-01-11 00:41:03','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/21/off-road-bits-part-1-chassis-chopping/',0,'post','',0);
