INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (166,1,'2007-05-11 15:12:37','2007-05-11 20:12:37','','off_road_step09.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step09jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:12:37','2007-05-11 20:12:37','',-1178913994,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step09.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(167,1,'2007-05-11 15:16:09','2007-05-11 20:16:09','','off_road_step10.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step10jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:16:09','2007-05-11 20:16:09','',-1178913994,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step10.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(168,1,'2007-05-11 15:20:40','2007-05-11 20:20:40','','off_road_step11.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step11jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:20:40','2007-05-11 20:20:40','',170,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step11.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(169,1,'2007-05-11 15:21:04','2007-05-11 20:21:04','','off_road_step12.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step12jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:21:04','2007-05-11 20:21:04','',170,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step12.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (170,9,'2004-05-22 15:17:00','2004-05-22 20:17:00','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step11.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step11.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step11.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step11.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nFirst, you need to find some stiff plastic that has a 90 degree bend. Make sure it\'s not brittle.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step12.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step12.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step12.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step12.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou need to make 2 holes in the motor/axle housing at roughly the level that the axle will sit. The holes I?m referring to are the ones at the top of this pic. This is the underside of the motor housing. Make sure not to drill into the axle clips, make the holes about 1mm in from each axle clip.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step13.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step13.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step13.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step13.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThis view of the front of the motor housing should clarify the position of the holes. You need to make a metal plate and drill 2 holes in it the same distance as the holes in the motor housing. You could use washers; you\'ll just need to trim one side of them. You\'ll see why when it comes together. And a couple of screws, the length is important. Again, you\'ll see how long they need to be once assembled.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step14.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step14.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step14.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step14.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou need to trim a piece of plastic into this shape, utilizing the bend. The dimensions are:\r\nLength 7mm\r\nWidth 17.5mm\r\nHeight 5.5mm\r\nNear enough is good enough though, the biggest consideration is to make sure it\'s no wider than the motor housing. Drill 2 holes in the bottom section, and 2 slots in the rear section, the same distance apart as the holes in the motor housing.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step15.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step15.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step15.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step15.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAttach the plastic mount by inserting the screws into the metal plate, then through the slots in the mounting into the motor housing. If you\'re using washers, trim one side and make that the bottom. This is because the slots allow adjustment of the height and angle of the motor housing, and washers may touch the bottom of the mounting, preventing adjustment.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step16.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step16.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step16.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step16.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou can just see the screws poking through into the axle area. This is why screw length is important. Too long and it will touch the axle, too short and it won\'t attach properly risking damage to the holes and failure of the connection. Find a couple of screws that screw into the holes in the mounting.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step17.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step17.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step17.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step17.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFind some thin flexible strong plastic, something like PVC from an inflatable toy. In this case I?m using plastic from a CD sleeve. Just so long as it\'s flexible enough to offer little resistance when bent, (although you could experiment with other plastic of varying thickness, etc) and resistant to tearing or splitting easily, like plastic packaging on biscuits or potato chips. I?ve found that plastic that doesn\'t stick to superglue is the stuff you want. Cut a piece about 14 x 17mm.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step18.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step18.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step18.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step18.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nMake 2 holes in the hinge and attach one end to the mounting. Make 2 large holes on the other side.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step19.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step19.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step19.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step19.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou need to make 2 holes in the chassis about 2 mm from the edge and sides. These should match up with the large holes in the hinge. The holes in the hinge need to be large to allow adjustment of the wheelbase length and the alignment.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step20.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step20.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step20.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step20.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFind or make a couple of washers that are larger than the holes in the hinge. And some screws that will screw into the chassis.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step21.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step21.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step21.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step21.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAttach the hinge to the chassis and you\'re ready to start on a shock.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step22.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step22.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step22.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step22.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nInside view. If you want you can use slightly longer screws on the chassis and have them screw into the battery terminals. That then makes the screws and washers the charging terminals.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step23.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step23.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step23.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step23.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see there\'s not a lot of room for error when choosing screw length for the motor housing/hinge mounting interface.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/23/off-road-bits-part-3-rear-shock/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 3: Rear Shock</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 2: Hinge Plate',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-2-hinge-plate','','','2008-01-10 18:40:46','2008-01-11 00:40:46','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/22/off-road-bits-part-2-hinge-plate/',0,'post','',0),(171,1,'2007-05-11 15:21:23','2007-05-11 20:21:23','','off_road_step13.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step13jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:21:23','2007-05-11 20:21:23','',-1178914619,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step13.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (180,1,'2007-05-11 15:25:32','2007-05-11 20:25:32','','off_road_step22.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step22jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:25:32','2007-05-11 20:25:32','',-1178914619,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step22.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(181,1,'2007-05-11 15:25:54','2007-05-11 20:25:54','','off_road_step23.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step23jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:25:54','2007-05-11 20:25:54','',-1178914619,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step23.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(182,1,'2007-05-11 15:32:13','2007-05-11 20:32:13','','off_road_step24.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step24jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:32:13','2007-05-11 20:32:13','',183,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step24.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(183,9,'2004-05-23 15:26:17','2004-05-23 20:26:17','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step24.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step24.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step24.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step24.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nYou\'ll need a screw first, roughly this size. This screw is from a bike valve, but the important thing is that the thread covers the bottom half of the shaft and that the top has a head.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step25.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step25.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step25.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step25.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFind some scrap plastic. If it has a hole in it that the screw can screw into, that would be easier but it\'s not necessary.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step26.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step26.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step26.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step26.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThat\'s all you need. Just a small block of plastic that screws onto the tip of the screw.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step27.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step27.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step27.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step27.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nCut a short section of tubing from a wd40 can, and find some strong wire such as a paperclip. The paperclip should move freely inside the tubing, but with minimal play.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step28.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step28.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step28.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step28.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nUsing a file, concrete, sand paper, whatever, shape the plastic block with a rounded end on one side and a bit of a groove cut at a 90 degree angle to the screw on the other. The groove is for the tubing to rest in.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step29.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step29.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step29.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step29.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nUsing blu tack as a jig, place the tubing horizontally, then the screw/plastic assembly on top vertically, with the groove in the plastic resting on the tubing. Make sure the screw and tubing are at right angles with each other, leaving the paperclip shaft in makes this easier. Apply good quality superglue to the plastic/tubing join and allow to dry. Don\'t let ANY glue go into the tubing.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step30.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step30.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step30.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step30.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nWhen dry, remove from the blu tack and apply more glue to the join and all the way around the tubing, being careful to avoid getting any glue inside the tubing.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step31.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step31.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step31.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step31.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSee? Lots\'a glue outside the tubing, none inside.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step32.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step32.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step32.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step32.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nI\'ve prepared this chassis for a dual cell. Find 2 short flat strips of plastic, and make sure the pivot rail (paperclip) is a little wider than the chassis, say 1.5 - 2mm.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step33.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step33.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step33.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step33.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nShape the plastic strips like this. A wide end and a narrow end. Make a hole in the narrow end that the pivot rail can fit snugly in.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step34.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step34.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step34.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step34.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nGlue a plastic strip onto the chassis by the wide end so the hole sits back about 1.5mm from the end of the chassis and up about .5mm.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step35.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step35.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step35.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step35.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSlide the pivot rail into the plastic strips through the holes and use the rail as a reference so the second plastic strip is glued straight. The rail must be straight.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step36.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step36.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step36.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step36.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSlide the shock onto the pivot rail, insert the rail into the holes and glue the rail in place at each end. Make sure the shock shaft (screw) is on top.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step37.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step37.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step37.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step37.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nInstall the rear end with the heat sink in and line up the shock to see roughly where the screw head lines up on the heat sink.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step38.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step38.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step38.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step38.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFind a strip of metal and bend it like this. You need to make 1 hole big enough for the shock shaft to fit through but not the screw head. The other hole is for a screw.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step39.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step39.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step39.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step39.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAttach the metal bit on the heat sink, making sure the shock mount sticks up where the screw head lined up. This heat sink is aluminum, so I can\'t get solder to stick to it. So I?ve screwed the shock mount on. But with a steel heat sink, I?d solder it in place.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step40.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step40.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step40.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step40.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nMore plastic. This is going to be the adjustment dial.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step41.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step41.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step41.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step41.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNearly there! Install the heat sink, and poke the screw through the shock mount from the rear.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step42.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step42.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step42.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step42.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSlide your spring over the screw, then screw on the adjustment dial.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step43.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step43.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step43.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step43.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nScrew the assembled shock into the pivot, and bang! You\'re done!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step44.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step44.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step44.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step44.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe assembled rear end, uncompressed.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step45.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step45.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step45.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step45.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n....and compressed. Preload is adjustable via the plastic dial.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step46.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step46.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step46.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step46.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTop, rear view.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step47.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step47.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step47.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step47.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nBottom left side. Now jam some FETS, a couple\'a batteries, and some knobbies onto this sucker and hit tha\' dirt!\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/24/off-road-bits-part-4-charging-solution/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 4: Charging Solution</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 3: Rear Shock',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-3-rear-shock','','','2008-01-10 18:40:26','2008-01-11 00:40:26','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/23/off-road-bits-part-3-rear-shock/',0,'post','',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (212,1,'2007-05-11 15:47:36','2007-05-11 20:47:36','','off_road_step53.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step53jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:47:36','2007-05-11 20:47:36','',214,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step53.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(213,1,'2007-05-11 15:47:49','2007-05-11 20:47:49','','off_road_step54.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step54jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:47:49','2007-05-11 20:47:49','',214,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step54.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(214,9,'2004-05-24 15:43:50','2004-05-24 20:43:50','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step48.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step48.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step48.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step48.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nThis is how I solve the problem of charging my chopped chassis bits. You could use a small dc plug and socket arrangement, but that would probably involve removing the shell when charging. I always dual cell my bits, but you could apply this idea to a single cell bit as well. BE VERY CAREFUL WHEN SOLDERING BATTERIES!! If heated enough they will explode, which ain\'t so great when your face is 30 cm away! At the very least you\'ll kill your battery so take care.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step49.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step49.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step49.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step49.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nConnect the + & - on one side of the batteries. Find some thin (but not too thin) metal and cut a couple of strips. Tin one end of each strip with solder.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step50.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step50.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step50.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step50.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTurn the batteries over and tin the other terminals. hold the metal strips with the tinned part touching the terminals and solder in place. be as quick as you can and cool the batteries by blowing on them immediately. bend the strips over, down the sides of the batteries.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step51.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step51.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step51.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step51.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nPlace the batteries in the chassis where you want them to sit. Hold them in place and mark the terminals through the holes in the chassis. pay careful attention to keep the orientation of the terminals the same, i.e. right side terminal is positive.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step52.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step52.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step52.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step52.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nMake holes where you marked the terminals, put a thin piece of plastic or something between the terminal and the battery to avoid damaging the battery. Find 2 screws and tap a thread into the holes you just made.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step53.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step53.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step53.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step53.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nConnect the batteries to the rx PCB by soldering. place them in the chassis, hold the hinge in place and insert a screw through the hinge, then the chassis, and into the battery terminal. You can see the screw just poking through between the terminal and battery in the above pic.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step54.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step54.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step54.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step54.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSo basically, the screws and washers that affix the hinge to the chassis double as charging terminals. As it won\'t fit on a standard charging plate anymore, I?ve made an adaptor that clips on so I can alternate between chopped and stock chassis\', shown in the pics below. Of course you could find a way to modify a charging plate to clip on without interfering with the suspension, but I gotta leave some things for others to figure out!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step55.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step55.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step55.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step55.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/25/off-road-bits-part-5-attaching-shells/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 5: Attaching Shells</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 4: Charging Solution',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-4-charging-solution','','','2008-01-10 18:40:08','2008-01-11 00:40:08','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/24/off-road-bits-part-4-charging-solution/',0,'post','',0),(215,1,'2007-05-11 15:48:03','2007-05-11 20:48:03','','off_road_step55.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step55jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:48:03','2007-05-11 20:48:03','',-1178916230,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step55.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (216,1,'2007-05-11 15:51:01','2007-05-11 20:51:01','','off_road_step56.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step56jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:51:01','2007-05-11 20:51:01','',218,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step56.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(217,1,'2007-05-11 15:51:27','2007-05-11 20:51:27','','off_road_step57.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step57jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:51:27','2007-05-11 20:51:27','',218,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step57.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(218,9,'2004-05-25 15:49:47','2004-05-25 20:49:47','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step56.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step56.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step56.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step56.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nThis isn\'t really a tutorial, just a few pics of what I do to attach shells to my dirty bits. This idea is taken from the method of attaching shells to some mini z chassis\'. I usually raise my shells so that means raising the shell tab on the front. I then glue some small pieces of foam rubber to the insides of the shell, so it grips the side of the chassis. You could glue the foam rubber to the chassis if you want, same result. the rear is then free to move. the rear shell tab on bit char g\'s and clones hits the shell on my hummer, but that\'s when the suspension has almost reached full travel so I don\'t mind. Not really a problem with an enertec, as they don\'t have the heat sink to hold the motor in place, they come with hardwired motors and a plastic cover. So the rear shell tab can be completely removed. I usually have to cut a little plastic from the front wheel wells to accommodate larger tires. You can see in the pics of my S2000 shell next to a stock one that I also removed a lot of the rear end to allow the suspension to move without touching the shell. But there\'s no rules, do what works for you.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step57.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step57.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step57.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step57.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/26/off-road-bits-part-6-front-suspension/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 6: Front Suspension</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 5: Attaching Shells',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-5-attaching-shells','','','2008-01-10 18:39:28','2008-01-11 00:39:28','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/25/off-road-bits-part-5-attaching-shells/',0,'post','',0),(219,1,'2007-05-11 15:54:02','2007-05-11 20:54:02','','off_road_step58.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step58jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:54:02','2007-05-11 20:54:02','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step58.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (220,1,'2007-05-11 15:54:17','2007-05-11 20:54:17','','off_road_step59.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step59jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:54:17','2007-05-11 20:54:17','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step59.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(221,1,'2007-05-11 15:54:28','2007-05-11 20:54:28','','off_road_step60.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step60jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:54:28','2007-05-11 20:54:28','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step60.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(222,1,'2007-05-11 15:54:41','2007-05-11 20:54:41','','off_road_step61.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step61jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:54:41','2007-05-11 20:54:41','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step61.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(223,1,'2007-05-11 15:54:53','2007-05-11 20:54:53','','off_road_step62.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step62jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:54:53','2007-05-11 20:54:53','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step62.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (224,1,'2007-05-11 15:55:06','2007-05-11 20:55:06','','off_road_step63.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step63jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:55:06','2007-05-11 20:55:06','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step63.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(225,1,'2007-05-11 15:55:19','2007-05-11 20:55:19','','off_road_step64.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step64jpg','','','2007-05-11 15:55:19','2007-05-11 20:55:19','',226,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step64.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(226,9,'2004-05-26 15:51:38','2004-05-26 20:51:38','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step58.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step58.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step58.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step58.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThis little walk through is mainly for enertec micro chargers as they have poor front suspension travel. But the same methods apply for any micro rc front end. You can see the knuckle as far up as it will go, but there\'s still room for more travel. The main things to avoid are that the knuckle doesn\'t pop out of the chassis at full compression, and that the steering arm doesn\'t pop off at full extension.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step59.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step59.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step59.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step59.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOne part to mod is the steering arm. Using a file remove some material from the underside, but not too much as it may weaken it and the less material the quicker it will wear. But I\'ve removed as much as half without a problem. Don\'t go any further in than necessary as the underside rests on the chassis at full extension, so mark it while assembled. Do your best to smooth the new surface down so the knuckles don\'t get caught on little burrs and nicks. I\'ve also removed some material from the front as the springs rub on it. Again, no further in than needed. I also like to bend the steering spring down a little at the ends to prevent it coming over the top of the adjustment peg, but it\'s up to you to decide whether that\'s necessary.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step60.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step60.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step60.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step60.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAnother thing I do to the steering spring is taking it off the little pegs, but leave it on the main one. Bend it around the main peg gently, a little at a time. This tightens the tension a little reducing the need for extra springs.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step61.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step61.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step61.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step61.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOn the knuckle hole in the chassis there\'s a slightly raised part. Removing this gives you a little more extension, but if you plan on using stiff springs that have no sag I\'d leave them.\r\n\r\nOn the underside of the steering cover are 2 raised parts that the steering arm rests on most of the time. Filing these down a little allows the steering arm to move up a little further.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step62.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step62.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step62.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step62.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe peg that goes into the steering arm can also be filed down a little. Make sure there are no burrs.\r\n\r\nWith springs, make sure the end of the top part sticks out a little. If it hugs the knuckle too close it will jam itself between the knuckle and steering cover, causing the steering to seize.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step63.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step63.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step63.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step63.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nIf you can\'t get hold of good springs you can do a poor man\'s version using springy plastic from packaging. This actually works quite well and you can experiment with different materials and thicknesses. And all made with scraps.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step64.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step64.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step64.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step64.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFirst, remove the stock sway bar and attach it to a piece of plastic using blu tack or tape. Then mark the outline on the plastic. Remove the sway bar and cut the new one out. Remember to make it a tiny bit bigger than it needs to be at first, you can always trim it down. Place the new sway bar where the old one was to make sure it fits. If you have to wedge it down trim it a little till it rests in sway bar groove flat. It should move freely but not so narrow that it twists around a lot. Once you\'ve cut it out you can bend the ends down or up to adjust tension, have a play around with it. Find some wire and cut a short section off. Place the sway bar in its groove and insert the wire in the holes where the old sway bar attached. Put a tiny amount of glue where the wire touches the spring to stop it slipping out and if needed a little bit to hold the wire in place. Just don\'t get any glue under the sway bar.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/27/off-road-bits-part-7-wheels/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 7: Wheels</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 6: Front Suspension',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-6-front-suspension','','','2008-01-10 18:39:07','2008-01-11 00:39:07','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/26/off-road-bits-part-6-front-suspension/',0,'post','',0),(227,1,'2007-05-11 16:00:34','2007-05-11 21:00:34','','off_road_step65.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step65jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:00:34','2007-05-11 21:00:34','',231,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step65.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (228,1,'2007-05-11 16:00:48','2007-05-11 21:00:48','','off_road_step66.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step66jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:00:48','2007-05-11 21:00:48','',231,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step66.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(229,1,'2007-05-11 16:01:02','2007-05-11 21:01:02','','off_road_step67.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step67jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:01:02','2007-05-11 21:01:02','',231,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step67.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(230,1,'2007-05-11 16:01:17','2007-05-11 21:01:17','','off_road_step68.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step68jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:01:17','2007-05-11 21:01:17','',231,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step68.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(231,9,'2004-05-27 15:57:25','2004-05-27 20:57:25','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step65.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step65.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step65.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step65.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThis time \'round we\'re gonna turn the humble Lego wheel into a very effective and (I think) aesthetically pleasing off-road wheel set. If you haven\'t already sold all your Lego as a teen to buy a triple chamber bong, then hunt down these types of wheels. And remember kiddies, drugs are bad, mm\'kaaay?!\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step66.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step66.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step66.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step66.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nRemove the axles (good luck, Lego made things to LAST!), and find some way of holding the wheel while you drill into it, like a vice. I just use a big Lego wheel to hold it, but this is pretty dangerous, so be careful. Find a drill bit that\'s as big as or bigger than the wheel diameter. You\'re going to drill into the side the axle was on, that\'s going to be the new outside.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step67.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step67.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step67.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step67.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nBasically you want to use the drill to countersink into the wheel. Don\'t go much further than this. Don\'t force the drill into it; just let the bit and weight of the drill do the work. just go a little at a time. If it\'s a bit lopsided, lean the drill towards the thicker side. Just remember, a bit at a time, if it takes you 5 min to do 1 wheel, that\'s better than destroying one in 5 seconds.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step68.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step68.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step68.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step68.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nYou\'ll need to make a hole in all 4 wheels, the size of the axle you plan on using. Get this as straight as you can. with a file or sand paper, file down the writing on the knob as level as you can on 3 of the wheels. put one aside, that will be the right rear wheel.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step69.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step69.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step69.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step69.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nWith the wheel that\'s still got the writing, you want to remove the knob that\'s protruding, so it\'s level with the main part, you can see what I mean with the right wheel in this pic. Again, try to smooth it down level.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step70.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step70.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step70.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step70.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFor the front wheels, I add a little bushing that\'s perfect for a front axle. And there\'s not enough plastic for the wheel to run true once attached to the knuckle. You could also use some tubing from a WD40 can; you may have to widen the internal diameter with a drill bit. do this by hand. You need to drill a hole the same diameter as the bushing you\'re using into the wheels. Poke the bushing into the (new) inside of the wheel as shown.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step71.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step71.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step71.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step71.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs I\'m using these bushings, I\'m drilling a hole the same diameter as the flanged part. The bushing is going to be the other way around when completed. But I leave it in this way as a guide when drilling. Do this with both front wheels. Tidy up the edges of the holes with a hobby knife and make sure the inside of the wheel is smooth and level.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step72.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step72.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step72.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step72.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAnd that\'s it. This is the outside of the wheels. front wheels top, left and right rear wheels bottom. These front wheels are a bit messy, but you get the idea. Just so long as the wheels run true.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step73.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step73.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step73.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step73.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n...and the inside, same order again. As you can see, the top right wheel is a bit wonky. It does run true, but you really want it to look like the top left one.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step74.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step74.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step74.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step74.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nHere are the rear wheels, mounted. You need to make a spacer about 1mm wide for the right side so they stick out the same amount both sides when attached to a chassis. The left wheel should be flush against the gear wheel, the gear wheel fits nicely inside the tire when it\'s on.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/28/off-road-bits-part-8-tires/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 8: Tires</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 7: Wheels',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-7-wheels','','','2008-01-10 18:38:50','2008-01-11 00:38:50','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/27/off-road-bits-part-7-wheels/',0,'post','',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (236,1,'2007-05-11 16:02:36','2007-05-11 21:02:36','','off_road_step73.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step73jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:02:36','2007-05-11 21:02:36','',-1178917045,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step73.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(237,1,'2007-05-11 16:02:50','2007-05-11 21:02:50','','off_road_step74.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step74jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:02:50','2007-05-11 21:02:50','',-1178917045,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step74.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(238,1,'2007-05-11 16:09:10','2007-05-11 21:09:10','','off_road_step75.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step75jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:09:10','2007-05-11 21:09:10','',243,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step75.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(239,1,'2007-05-11 16:09:25','2007-05-11 21:09:25','','off_road_step76.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step76jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:09:25','2007-05-11 21:09:25','',243,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step76.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0);
INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (240,1,'2007-05-11 16:09:37','2007-05-11 21:09:37','','off_road_step77.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step77jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:09:37','2007-05-11 21:09:37','',243,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step77.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(241,1,'2007-05-11 16:09:51','2007-05-11 21:09:51','','off_road_step78.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step78jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:09:51','2007-05-11 21:09:51','',243,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step78.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(242,1,'2007-05-11 16:10:04','2007-05-11 21:10:04','','off_road_step79.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step79jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:10:04','2007-05-11 21:10:04','',243,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step79.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(243,9,'2004-05-28 16:06:05','2004-05-28 21:06:05','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step75.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step75.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step75.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step75.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThese are the tires I\'ve been using, but use whatever you want. Mount the tires on some wheels & axles to make it easier to hold. The 2 most important tools for this job are:\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nA new, razor sharp scalpel or hobby knife, small is good. Ideally you want a curved and a straight blade, but either will do.\r\n\r\nPATIENCE!! Be prepared to ruin a few tires before you get this right. Start on some crappy ones.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step76.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step76.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step76.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step76.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNotice how there\'s 2 halves to the tire. Divide each half into thirds. Start the first cut 2/3 of the way into the outside half. Cut around the diameter of the tire, but no deeper than the actual chevron (chevrons = the knobby bits).\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step77.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step77.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step77.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step77.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nStart 1/3 of the way in this time and one by one, cut at about a 45 degree angle towards the first cut so the 2 cuts meet up at the base of the chevron, no deeper. So you\'re removing the middle third. You can see one of the middle bits on the scalpel blade.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step78.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step78.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step78.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step78.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nRepeat the procedure for the inner half of the tire. This is a finished rear tire. Angle the cuts whichever way you want, I find that this design with 4 sharp edges facing outwards provides the best cornering traction.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step79.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step79.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step79.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step79.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nFor the front tires, do the same with the outer half. Then make a cut in the middle of the tire, around the diameter of the wheel. Again, just cut as deep as the chevrons, this cut is a guide for the next step.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step80.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step80.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step80.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step80.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThis is where the straight edged blade comes in. hold the wheels sideways, and cut straight down into the inner side of the tire to the halfway point you marked, NO FURTHER! Then cut sideways along the halfway point and meet up with the vertical cut. Go around the whole tire like this, a bit at a time until it\'s round all the way. I do this to prevent the front tires from rubbing on the chassis when turning.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step81.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step81.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step81.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step81.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nOnce you\'ve evened them up and mounted them on my custom wheels, you\'ll notice that they stick out a little on the inside compared to the ones on the car. I just trim this flush with the wheel, mainly for aesthetics, but it doesn\'t need to be there and may rub on the chassis.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step82.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step82.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step82.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step82.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n...like this!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step83.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step83.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step83.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step83.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see, the tire would have rubbed on the chassis if it were not cut down.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step84.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step84.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step84.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step84.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nThe finished products! These tires excel on concrete, but are suitable for most outdoor and off-road applications.\r\n\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/29/off-road-bits-part-9-ramps/\">Next: Off-Road Bits - Part 9: Ramps</a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 8: Tires',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-8-tires','','','2008-01-10 18:38:35','2008-01-11 00:38:35','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/28/off-road-bits-part-8-tires/',0,'post','',0);

INSERT INTO `wp_avai7v_posts` (`ID`,`post_author`,`post_date`,`post_date_gmt`,`post_content`,`post_title`,`post_category`,`post_excerpt`,`post_status`,`comment_status`,`ping_status`,`post_password`,`post_name`,`to_ping`,`pinged`,`post_modified`,`post_modified_gmt`,`post_content_filtered`,`post_parent`,`guid`,`menu_order`,`post_type`,`post_mime_type`,`comment_count`) VALUES (252,1,'2007-05-11 16:16:39','2007-05-11 21:16:39','','off_road_step87.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step87jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:16:39','2007-05-11 21:16:39','',254,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step87.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(253,1,'2007-05-11 16:17:04','2007-05-11 21:17:04','','off_road_step88.jpg',0,'','inherit','open','open','','off_road_step88jpg','','','2007-05-11 16:17:04','2007-05-11 21:17:04','',254,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step88.jpg',0,'attachment','image/jpeg',0),(254,9,'2004-05-30 16:15:41','2004-05-30 21:15:41','<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step87.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step87.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step87.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step87.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\nWith all the outdoor driving and jumping my bits are getting bashed up reel good! So to protect the front end from getting too scratched up (and mainly for looks!) I used the metal off a 1.4mb floppy to make skid pans. You could use an aluminum can if you want just so long as whatever you use is not magnetically attractive, or the steering will be rendered useless. This stuff can be cut with scissors and bent easily. I just glued them in place with good quality super glue.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step88.jpg\' title=\'off_road_step88.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/off_road_step88.jpg\' alt=\'off_road_step88.jpg\' /></a>','Off-Road Bits - Part 10: Skid Pans',0,'','publish','open','open','','off-road-bits-part-10-skid-pans','','','2008-01-10 18:38:03','2008-01-11 00:38:03','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/05/30/off-road-bits-part-10-skid-pans/',0,'post','',0),(255,1,'2004-08-08 18:52:52','2004-08-08 23:52:52','There\'s been so many questions about the mosfet mod.\r\nFrom where to buy the chips or the mod, who supplies the chips or who can do the mod, etc., etc.\r\nSo here\'s an article that put\'s it all into perspective.\r\n\r\n<strong>What does \"FET\" stand for?</strong> (as told by Bird)\r\n\"FET\" is the shortened slang word for \"MOSFET\"... Metal Oxide Semiconductor Field Effect Transistor.\r\nBasically, all of the drive circuits on these cars are controlled through transistors... regular, run of the mill, low-grade transistors... People use MOSFETS as an upgrade, because they have a lower resistance than the stock transistors, and allow more current and voltage to reach the motor... getting the most out of ordinary motors AND allowing you to upgrade to \"hotter\" motors with higher current/voltage draw.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<strong>What does the \"FET\" mod do?</strong> (as told by cj)\r\nLet me use water with my analogy. A FET or Transistor is like a valve you attach your water hose to. An water valve is basically a ball with a hole drilled in it to let water pass though. The larger the Valve the lager the hole or lower the resistance.\r\nImagine your Batteries are a Huge tank of water and you need to fill a 10,000 Gallon pool (motor). The wires from your tank to the FET(Valve) are like by default garden hose sized. the ones from your FET(Valve) to the pool(Motor) are the same size. If that was the stock set up you would know about how fast you would fill that pool. Now lets say we upgrade the Wire(hoses) and FET(valve) with say a Nelly. Now you have a Fire hose running from the Tank to the FET(valve) and a fire hose running from the FET(valve) to the Pool(Motor). With this way you could swim within the hour or so instead of a day or so.\r\n\r\n<strong>How hard is it to do the mod?</strong>\r\nDifficult. If you can\'t read and understand the directions posted in our forums, then you shouldn\'t attempt the mod. You need solid electrical and soldering skills.\r\n\r\n<strong>Where can I buy the \"fets\" for the mod?</strong>\r\nNot at your local hobby store. You\'ll find them at an electronics supply store like Mouser or Digikey.\r\n\r\n<strong>Who on this site can do the \"fet\" mod for me and where are they located?</strong>\r\ncrazydave - US\r\ncodesuidae - US\r\nSuperFly or hue35 - US\r\nph2t - Australia\r\nMicro_Amps - Australia\r\n(let me know if I\'m missing someone)\r\n\r\n<strong>Got any information about \"fets\"?</strong>\r\nCertainly, lots, probably the most you\'ll find. Check the forums.\r\n\r\nMosfet Diagrams:\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=1778\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=1778</a>\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2005/03/17/zipzap-se-mosfet-mod/\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2005/03/17/zipzap-se-mosfet-mod/</a>\r\n\r\nMosfet In ZZ SE:\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=2140\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=2140</a>\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2005/03/17/zipzap-se-mosfet-mod/\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2005/03/17/zipzap-se-mosfet-mod/</a>\r\n\r\nMosfet Improvement (biff):\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=3594\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=3594</a>\r\n\r\n\"Woah Nelly\" Turbo for 1:2x scale\r\n<a href=\"http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=3612\" target=\"_blank\">http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/pages/phpForum/viewtopic.php?t=3612</a>','Mosfet Mod: Where, Who, How, etc.',0,'','publish','open','open','','mosfet-mod-where-who-how-etc','','','2008-01-10 18:37:31','2008-01-11 00:37:31','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2004/08/08/mosfet-mod-where-who-how-etc/',0,'post','',0);

Without further ado...\r\n\r\nFirst off you have to remove the wheels off of an Import Tunerz diecast. You can change this up for other brand diecasts, but I like the style of the Import Tunerz best. That and they are always the perfect size.\r\n\r\n<!--more-->\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step01.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step01.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step01.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step01.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNext you want to strip down the rear axel of your Zip/Bit (for this tutorial I am using a Zip Zap SE) Taking off the wheels on the rear is as simple as twisting them off. Note that for the side without the gear it is best to pre-cut the wheel off on the axel so that a little bit of plastic is left over. See the fuzzy pic.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step02.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step02.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step02.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step02.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAfter I decided it was best to remove the nub off of the gear so that the wheel would attach flat.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step03.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step03.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step03.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step03.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nTo make it so the Import Tunerz wheels fit you do have to shorten the axel a bit. In the past I was able to make it so that the rear wheels didn\'t need to have holes all the way through them, but with this particular style it wasn\'t possible.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step04.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step04.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step04.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step04.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSpeaking of wich, now it is time to drill all 4 of your new wheels. Make sure you use a bit that is the right diamiter for your axels. Like I said, it is possible to have it so the rear wheels only need their hole widened to punctured all the way through. Note my Son\'s hands on these 2 pics, yeah I\'m a bit sweat shop.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step05.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step05.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step05.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step05.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAfter drilling the wheels you will want to shave the nub off of one of your rear wheels so it will be flush w/ the gear.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step06.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step06.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step06.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step06.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nPop on your tires now. Note that there is no grove, personally that doesn\'t bother me, but if it does you can form one by glueing wire around the wheel.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step07.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step07.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step07.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step07.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow for the front wheels. Take them off and pop out those axels, use whatever method you prefer.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step08.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step08.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step08.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step08.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nAs you can see I used two different methods, 1st, beating the shit out of it till I could cut her out, and then I woke up and used the soldering iron method. (That\'s when you heat the axel and melt the surronding plastic.)\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step09.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step09.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step09.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step09.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNow it\'s time to put it all together. Use epoxy and attach the rear wheels, making sure they are flush as to not add wobble. For the fronts it depnds on if you are doing a Zip, Bit, or SE. For me I applied the epoxy inside the wheel, but for Zips and Bits you apply it inside the arm. Let them set over night.\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step10.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step10.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step10.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step10.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nSlap it all together, and Bam, you have insta Bling!\r\n\r\n<a href=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step11.jpg\' title=\'import_racerz_step11.jpg\'><img src=\'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/wp-content/uploads/2007/05/import_racerz_step11.jpg\' alt=\'import_racerz_step11.jpg\' /></a>\r\n\r\nNote the lowered pcb on this momma jomma!','Import Racerz Wheel Mod',0,'','publish','open','open','','import-racerz-wheel-mod','','','2007-08-19 20:30:13','2007-08-20 01:30:13','',0,'http://bitpimps.lixlink.com/articles/2005/02/06/import-racerz-wheel-mod/',0,'post','',1);
